{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\defeq{{\{\,\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak f({\mathrm{X}})\in{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}\allowbreak\,\}}}

and
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}\defeq{{\{\,\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak f({\mathrm{X}})\in{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}\allowbreak\,\}}}
.
We have that both
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\sqsubseteq{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}
and

{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}\sqsubseteq{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
hold.
Thus, by inductive hypothesis, for every
({\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}},{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}})\in{\mathsf{par}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\allowbreak\right)}
it holds true that
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}\neq\emptyset
and

{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\varphi_{1}
or it holds true that

{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}\neq\emptyset
and
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\varphi_{2}
, which amounts to
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
.

On the other hand, we also have, by semantics,
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
if and only if
{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
.
By inductive hypothesis and Proposition
, this implies

{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
, which amounts to

{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
.

–

(
\varphi=\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}
)
We have, by semantics,
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
if
and only if there is
({\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}},{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}})\in{\mathsf{par}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\allowbreak\right)}

such that
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}\neq\emptyset
implies
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\varphi_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}\neq\emptyset

implies
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\varphi_{2}
.
Now, let
({\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}},{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}})\in{\mathsf{par}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)}
, where

{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\defeq{{\{\,\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak f({\mathrm{X}})\in{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}\allowbreak\,\}}}

and
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}\defeq{{\{\,\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak f({\mathrm{X}})\in{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}\allowbreak\,\}}}
.
We have that both
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\sqsubseteq{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}
and

{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}\sqsubseteq{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
hold.
Moreover, we have that
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}=\emptyset
implies
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}=\emptyset
and
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}=\emptyset
implies
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}=\emptyset
.
In addition, by inductive hypothesis,
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\varphi_{1}
implies
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\varphi_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\varphi_{2}
implies
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\varphi_{2}
.
Therefore,
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
holds.

On the other hand, we also have, by semantics,
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
if and only if
{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
.
By inductive hypothesis and Proposition
, this implies

{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
, which amounts to

{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
.

–

(
\varphi=\exists\apElm\colon\!\spcElm\ldotp\psi
)
We have, by semantics,
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
if
and only if
{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\psi
.
By inductive hypothesis and Proposition
, this implies

{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\psi
, which
amounts to
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
.

On the other hand, we also have, by semantics,
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
if and only if
{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
.
By inductive hypothesis and Proposition
, this implies

{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
, which amounts to

{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
.

–

(
\varphi=\forall\apElm\colon\!\spcElm\ldotp\psi
)
We have, by semantics,
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
if
and only if
{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\psi
.
By inductive hypothesis and Proposition
, this implies

{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\psi
, which
amounts to
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
.

On the other hand, we also have, by semantics,
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
if and only if
{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
.
By inductive hypothesis and Proposition
, this implies

{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
, which amounts to

{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
.∎

Theorem 0

(Double Dualization)
.

Let
\varphi
be a
GFG
-
Q
PTL
formula and
{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}_{\subseteq}}({\mathsf{free}\!\left(\allowbreak\varphi\allowbreak\right)})
.
Then,
{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}\varphi

iff

{{\overline{{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\varphi

iff

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\varphi
.

Proof.

The fact that
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\varphi

iff

{{\overline{{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\varphi
immediately follows from
{\mathfrak{X}}\equiv{{\overline{{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}}}}
(Proposition
) and
Corollary
.

We turn now on proving that
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\varphi

iff

{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}\varphi
, for all
{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}_{\subseteq}}({\mathsf{free}\!\left(\allowbreak\varphi\allowbreak\right)})
.
The proof is by induction on the structure of the formula.

(base case)

If
\varphi\in\text{LTL$$}
, then the claim follows immediately from the
semantics and Lemma
.

(inductive step)

If
\varphi=\neg\psi
, then we have:
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathfrak{X}}{{{\,\not\models}^{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}}\>\psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny ind.hp.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\not\models}^{\alpha}}\>\psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}\>\varphi
.

If
\varphi=\varphi_{1}\wedge\varphi_{2}
, then we have:

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny Thm.\penalty\ \ref{thm:dlt}}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
; and

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
.

If
\varphi=\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}
, then we have:

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
; and

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny Thm.\penalty\ \ref{thm:dlt}}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
.

If
\varphi=\exists\apElm\colon\!\spcElm\ldotp\psi
, then we have:

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny Thm.\penalty\ \ref{thm:dlt}}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
; and

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
.

If
\varphi=\forall\apElm\colon\!\spcElm\ldotp\psi
, then we have:

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi
;

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny Thm.\penalty\ \ref{thm:dlt}}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
. ∎

Lemma 0

(Boolean Laws)
.

Let
\varphi
,
\varphi_{1}
,
\varphi_{2}
be
GFG
-
Q
PTL
formulae:

1.

\varphi\equiv\neg\neg\varphi
;

2.

\varphi_{1}\wedge\varphi_{2}\Rightarrow\varphi_{1}
;

3.

\varphi_{1}\Rightarrow\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}
;

4.

\varphi_{1}\!\wedge\!(\varphi\!\wedge\!\varphi_{2})\equiv(\varphi_{1}\!\wedge\!\varphi)\!\wedge\!\varphi_{2}
;

5.

\varphi_{1}\!\vee\!(\varphi\!\vee\!\varphi_{2})\equiv(\varphi_{1}\!\vee\!\varphi)\!\vee\!\varphi_{2}
;

6.

\varphi_{1}\wedge\varphi_{2}\equiv\neg(\neg\varphi_{1}\vee\neg\varphi_{2})
;

7.

\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}\equiv\neg(\neg\varphi_{1}\wedge\neg\varphi_{2})
;

8.

\exists^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\varphi\equiv\neg(\forall^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\neg\varphi)
;

9.

\forall^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\varphi\equiv\neg(\exists^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\neg\varphi)
.

Proof.

Thanks to Corollary
, it suffices to prove the
equivalence for
\equiv^{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in\{\exists\forall,\forall\exists\}
.
Let
\varphi
be a
Q
PTL
formula and
{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}({\mathsf{free}\!\left(\allowbreak\varphi\allowbreak\right)})
a hyperassignment.

)

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\neg\neg\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\not\models}^{\forall\exists}}\neg\varphi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi
;

-
)

Trivial and omitted.

)

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\varphi_{1}\wedge\varphi_{2}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\forall({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathfrak{X}_{2}})\in{\mathsf{par}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}
it holds

({\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\neq\emptyset
and
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\varphi_{1})
or
({\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\neq\emptyset
and
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\varphi_{2})\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny 1.,sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\forall({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathfrak{X}_{2}})\in{\mathsf{par}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}
it holds
({\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\neq\emptyset
and
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}{{\,\not\models}^{\forall\exists}}\neg\varphi_{1})
or
({\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\neq\emptyset
and
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\not\models}^{\forall\exists}}\neg\varphi_{2})\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\not\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\neg\varphi_{1}\vee\neg\varphi_{2}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\neg(\neg\varphi_{1}\vee\neg\varphi_{2})
;

)

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\exists({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathfrak{X}_{2}})\in{\mathsf{par}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}
s.t.

({\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\neq\emptyset
implies
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\varphi_{1})
and
({\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\neq\emptyset
implies
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\varphi_{2})\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny 1.,sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\exists({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathfrak{X}_{2}})\in{\mathsf{par}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}
s.t.

({\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\neq\emptyset
implies
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}{{\,\not\models}^{\exists\forall}}\neg\varphi_{1})
and
({\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\neq\emptyset
implies
{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}{{\,\not\models}^{\exists\forall}}\neg\varphi_{2})\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\not\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\neg\varphi_{1}\wedge\neg\varphi_{2}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\neg(\neg\varphi_{1}\wedge\neg\varphi_{2})
;

)

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\exists\apElm\colon\!\spcElm\ldotp\psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny 1.,sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\not\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\neg\psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\not\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\forall\apElm\colon\!\spcElm\ldotp\neg\psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\neg\forall\apElm\colon\!\spcElm\ldotp\neg\psi
;

)

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\forall\apElm\colon\!\spcElm\ldotp\psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny 1.,sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\not\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\neg\psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\not\models}^{\exists\forall}}\>\exists\apElm\colon\!\spcElm\ldotp\neg\psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny sem.}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\>\neg\exists\apElm\colon\!\spcElm\ldotp\neg\psi
.
∎

Proposition 0
.

Let
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\!\in\!{\mathrm{HAsg}}
with
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
and
\qntElm\!\in\!{\mathrm{Qn}}
:
{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\qntElm)\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\qntElm)
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by induction on the length of the quantification prefix

\qntElm
.

If
\qntElm=\varepsilon
(base case), then we have

{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\qntElm)={\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\sqsubseteq{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}={\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\qntElm)
.

If
\qntElm={\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm.\qntElm^{\prime}
(inductive step), then we
distinguish two cases.

•

If
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are coherent, then we have

{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\qntElm)={\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},\qntElm^{\prime})
and

{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\qntElm)={\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},\qntElm^{\prime})
.
By Proposition
,
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\sqsubseteq{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
implies

{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
and, by inductive hypothesis,

{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},\qntElm^{\prime})\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},\qntElm^{\prime})
, hence the
thesis.

•

If
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are not coherent, then we have

{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\qntElm)={\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},\qntElm^{\prime})

and
{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\qntElm)={\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},\qntElm^{\prime})
.
By Proposition
,
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\sqsubseteq{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
implies
{{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}}\sqsubseteq{{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}}
, and, by
inductive hypothesis,
{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},\qntElm^{\prime})\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},\qntElm^{\prime})
,
hence the thesis. ∎

Lemma 0

(Prefix Evolution)
.

Let
\qntElm\phi
be a
GFG
-
Q
PTL
formula with
\qntElm\!\in\!{\mathrm{Qn}}
.
Then,
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\qntElm\phi

iff

{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm){{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\phi
, for all
{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}({\mathsf{free}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\phi\allowbreak\right)})
.

Proof.

(base case)

When
\qntElm=\varepsilon
the claim is trivial.

(inductive step)

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\exists^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{evl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},\qntElm){{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{evl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},\exists^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm){{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\psi
.

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\exists^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\exists^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{evl}_{\forall\exists}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},\qntElm){{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{evl}_{\forall\exists}}({\mathfrak{X}},\exists^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm){{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\psi
.

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\forall^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\forall^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}}{{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{evl}_{\exists\forall}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},\qntElm){{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{evl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},\forall^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm){{\,\models}^{\exists\forall}}\psi
.

–

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\forall^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}{{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\qntElm\ldotp\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{evl}_{\forall\exists}}({\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},\qntElm){{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\psi\Leftrightarrow{\mathsf{evl}_{\forall\exists}}({\mathfrak{X}},\forall^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm){{\,\models}^{\forall\exists}}\psi
.
∎

We now introduce an alternative to
{\mathsf{evl}}
that we use to prove theorems presented in this paper. To use this alternative, we prove propositions that have not been expressed in the main article.

Proposition 2
.

Let
{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}({\mathrm{P}})
with
{\mathrm{P}}\!\subseteq\!{\mathrm{AP}}
,
\spcElm\in{\mathrm{\mathbbo{\Theta}}}
,
\apElm\in{\mathrm{AP}}\!\setminus\!{\mathrm{P}}
, and
\Psi\subseteq{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathrm{P}}\cup\{\apElm\})
.
There exists
{\mathrm{W}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
such
that
{\mathrm{W}}\subseteq\Psi

iff
the following conditions hold true:

1.

there exist
{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathrm{P}})
and
{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}

such that
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\subseteq\Psi
,
whenever
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are coherent;

2.

for all
{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathrm{P}})
, there is
{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}

such that
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\subseteq\Psi
,
whenever
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are not coherent.

Proof.

If
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are coherent, then
{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)={\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}={{{\{\,\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}},{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})\allowbreak\,\}}}}
, and we have:
\exists{\mathrm{W}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)\ .\ {\mathrm{W}}\subseteq\Psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\exists{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathrm{P}})\ \exists{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}\ .\ {\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\subseteq\Psi
.

If
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are not coherent, then we proceed as follows:
\exists{\mathrm{W}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)\ .\ {\mathrm{W}}\subseteq\Psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\exists{\mathrm{W}}\in{{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}}\ .\ {\mathrm{W}}\subseteq\Psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\exists{\mathsf{\Gamma}}\in{\mathrm{Chc}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\right)}\ .\ {\mathsf{img}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{\Gamma}}\allowbreak\right)}={{\{\,\allowbreak{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathrm{Z}})\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak{\mathrm{Z}}\in{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\,\}}}\subseteq\Psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\exists{\mathsf{\Gamma}}\in{\mathrm{Chc}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\right)}\ .\ \forall{\mathrm{Z}}\in{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\ .\ {\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathrm{Z}})\in\Psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\forall{\mathrm{Z}}\in{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\ .\ \exists\asgElm[{\mathrm{Z}}]\in{\mathrm{Z}}\ .\ \asgElm[{\mathrm{Z}}]\in\Psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\forall{\mathsf{G}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathrm{P}})\ .\ \forall{\mathrm{Y}}\in{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}\ .\ \exists\asgElm[{\mathsf{G}},{\mathrm{Y}}]\in{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{Y}},{\mathsf{G}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\ .\ \asgElm[{\mathsf{G}},{\mathrm{Y}}]\in\Psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\forall{\mathsf{G}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathrm{P}})\ .\ \forall\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Chc}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\ .\ \exists\asgElm[{\mathsf{G}},\Lambda]\in{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{img}\!\left(\allowbreak\Lambda\allowbreak\right)},{\mathsf{G}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\ .\ \asgElm[{\mathsf{G}},\Lambda]\in\Psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\forall{\mathsf{G}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathrm{P}})\ .\ \forall\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Chc}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\ .\ \exists\asgElm[{\mathsf{G}},\Lambda]\in{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\{\,\allowbreak\Lambda({\mathrm{X}})\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}},{\mathsf{G}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\ .\ \asgElm[{\mathsf{G}},\Lambda]\in\Psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\forall{\mathsf{G}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathrm{P}})\ .\ \forall\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Chc}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\ .\ \exists{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}\ .\ {\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\Lambda({\mathrm{X}}),{\mathsf{G}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\in\Psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\forall{\mathsf{G}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathrm{P}})\ .\ \exists{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}\ .\ \forall\asgElm\in{\mathrm{X}}\ .\ {\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\asgElm,{\mathsf{G}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\in\Psi\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}}\forall{\mathsf{G}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathrm{P}})\ .\ \exists{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}\ .\ {\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{G}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\subseteq\Psi
.
∎

We now introduce
{\mathsf{nevl}}
. First, we define it only on
{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm
.

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)\defeq{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
, if
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are coherent, and
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)\defeq{{\{\,\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth,\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\eth\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}
, otherwise, where
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth,\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\defeq\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathsf{dom}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth\allowbreak\right)}}{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth({\mathsf{F}}),{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
.

Proposition 3
.

If
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\!\equiv\!{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
then
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)\equiv{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
, for all

{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}
,
{\mathtt{Q}}\in\{\exists,\forall\}
,
\spcElm\in{\mathrm{\mathbbo{\Theta}}}
, and
\apElm\in{\mathrm{AP}}\setminus{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}
.

Proof.

Assume
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
to be coherent.
Then,
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)={\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\equiv{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}={\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
.

Assume, now,
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
not to be coherent.
Then,
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)={{\{\,\allowbreak\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})}{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth({\mathsf{F}}),{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\eth\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\,\}}}
.

In order to prove that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
, we let
\Psi\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
and we show that there is
W\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
with
W\subseteq\Psi
.
By the definition of
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
, we
have that
\Psi=\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})}{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth({\mathsf{F}}),{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
for some
\eth\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}
.
This means that for every
{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})
there
is
{\mathrm{X}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}\defeq\eth({\mathsf{F}})\in{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}
such that

{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}_{{\mathsf{F}}},{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\subseteq\Psi
.
By Proposition
, there exists
{\mathrm{W}}^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
such that
{\mathrm{W}}^{\prime}\subseteq\Psi
.
Since, due to Proposition
,
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\equiv{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}

implies
{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)\equiv{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
, we have that there is
{\mathrm{W}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
such that
{\mathrm{W}}\subseteq{\mathrm{W}}^{\prime}\subseteq\Psi
.

In order to prove the converse,
i.e.
,
{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
, let
\Psi\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
.
By instantiating
{\mathrm{W}}
with
\Psi
in Proposition
, we have
that for all
{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\allowbreak\right)})
, there is

{\mathrm{X}}^{\prime}_{{\mathsf{F}}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
such that
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}^{\prime}_{{\mathsf{F}}},{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\subseteq\Psi
.
By
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\equiv{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
, we have that there is
{\mathrm{X}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}
such that
{\mathrm{X}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}\subseteq{\mathrm{X}}^{\prime}_{{\mathsf{F}}}
, which, in turn,
implies
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}_{{\mathsf{F}}},{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\subseteq{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}^{\prime}_{{\mathsf{F}}},{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\subseteq\Psi
.
Now, define
\eth
as
\eth({\mathsf{F}})\defeq{\mathrm{X}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}
for
every
{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})={\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\allowbreak\right)})
.
Clearly, both of the following hold:
\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})}{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth({\mathsf{F}}),{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
and
\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})}{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth({\mathsf{F}}),{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\subseteq\Psi
; hence the thesis.
∎

We extend the definition of
{\mathsf{nevl}}
to any quantifier prefix.

•

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\epsilon)\defeq{\mathfrak{X}}
;

•

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm)\defeq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm),\qntElm)
.

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm)\defeq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}(\{\{{{\varnothing}}\}\},\qntElm)

Functions
{\mathsf{evl}}
and
{\mathsf{nevl}}
preserve equivalence between
hyperassignments, as stated in the next proposition.
Thus, in what follows, we will use the two functions interchangeably, as they
are only involved in functions and relations that are invariant with respect to
equivalence between hyperassignments.

Proposition 4
.

If
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\!\equiv\!{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
then
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\qntElm)\!\equiv\!{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\qntElm)
, for all
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}
and
\qntElm\in{\mathrm{Qn}}
, with
{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\cap{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by induction on the length of the quantification prefix

\qntElm
.

(base case) If
\qntElm=\varepsilon
, then we have

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\qntElm)={\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\equiv{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}={\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\qntElm)
.

(inductive step) If
\qntElm={\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm.\qntElm^{\prime}
, then

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\qntElm)={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm),\qntElm^{\prime})
.
The thesis follows by a straightforward application of
Proposition
and the inductive hypothesis.
∎

Let
{\mathrm{P}},{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\mathrm{AP}}
,
\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathrm{P}})
, and
{\mathrm{X}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathrm{P}})
.
We define
(\asgElm\setminus{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime})\defeq\asgElm\rst\,({\mathrm{P}}\setminus{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime})
and
{\mathrm{X}}\setminus{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime}\defeq{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\setminus{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}
.
Notice that
{\mathrm{X}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Y}}
implies
{\mathrm{X}}\setminus\{\apElm\}\subseteq{\mathrm{Y}}\setminus\{\apElm\}
, for all
{\mathrm{X}},{\mathrm{Y}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathrm{P}})
,
{\mathrm{P}}\subseteq{\mathrm{AP}}
, and
\apElm\in{\mathrm{P}}
, and

(\bigcup_{{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}}{\mathrm{X}})\setminus\{\apElm\}=\bigcup_{{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}}({\mathrm{X}}\setminus\{\apElm\})
for all
{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}({\mathrm{P}})
,

{\mathrm{P}}\subseteq{\mathrm{AP}}
, and
\apElm\in{\mathrm{P}}
.

Proposition 5
.

{\mathrm{Y}}\setminus{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}\subseteq\bigcup{\mathfrak{X}}
, for all
{\mathrm{Y}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)
,
{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}({\mathrm{P}})
, and

\qntElm\in{\mathrm{Qn}}
, with
{\mathrm{P}}\subseteq{\mathrm{AP}}
and
{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}\cap{\mathrm{P}}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by induction on the length of the quantification prefix

\qntElm
.

(base case) If
\qntElm=\varepsilon
, then we have

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)={\mathfrak{X}}
, and the claim follows trivially.

If
\qntElm={\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm
, let
{\mathrm{Y}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)
.
If
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are coherent, then
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)={\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
.
Thus,
{\mathrm{Y}}={\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
for some
{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}
and

{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})
.
Trivially,
{\mathrm{Y}}\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak p\allowbreak\allowbreak\,\}}}={\mathrm{X}}
, and we are done.
If, on the other hand,
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are not coherent, then

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)={{\{\,\allowbreak\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})}{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth({\mathsf{F}}),{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\eth\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}
.
Thus,
{\mathrm{Y}}=\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})}{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth({\mathsf{F}}),{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
for some
\eth\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}}
.
Clearly,
{\mathrm{Y}}\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\apElm\allowbreak\allowbreak\,\}}}=\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})}\eth({\mathsf{F}})
.
Since
\eth({\mathsf{F}})\in{\mathfrak{X}}
for every
{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})
, it holds that
\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})}\eth({\mathsf{F}})\subseteq\bigcup{\mathfrak{X}}
, hence the
thesis.

(inductive step) If
\qntElm={\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm.\qntElm^{\prime}
(with

\qntElm^{\prime}\neq\varepsilon
), then
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm),\qntElm^{\prime})
.
Let
{\mathrm{Y}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)
.
By inductive hypothesis,
{\mathrm{Y}}\setminus{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm^{\prime}\allowbreak\right)}\subseteq\bigcup_{{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)}{\mathrm{X}}
.
Since by inductive hypothesis it also holds that
{\mathrm{X}}\setminus\{\apElm\}\subseteq\bigcup{\mathfrak{X}}
for every
{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
, we have that
\bigcup_{{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)}({\mathrm{X}}\setminus\{\apElm\})\subseteq\bigcup{\mathfrak{X}}
.
Therefore, it holds
{\mathrm{Y}}\setminus{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}=({\mathrm{Y}}\setminus{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm^{\prime}\allowbreak\right)})\setminus\{\apElm\}\subseteq(\bigcup_{{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)}{\mathrm{X}})\setminus\{\apElm\}=\bigcup_{{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)}({\mathrm{X}}\setminus\{\apElm\})\subseteq\bigcup{\mathfrak{X}}
.
∎

-C

Proofs for Section
IV

A
path

\pthElm\!\in\!{\mathrm{Pth}}\!\subseteq\!{\mathrm{Ps}^{\infty}}
is a
finite or infinite sequence of positions compatible with the move relation,

i.e.
,
((\pthElm)_{i},(\pthElm)_{i+1})\in{\mathit{Mv}}
, for all
i\in{{[0\allowbreak,\allowbreak{{\lvert\allowbreak\pthElm\allowbreak\rvert}}-1)}}
; it is
initial
if
{{\lvert\allowbreak\pthElm\allowbreak\rvert}}>0

and
{\mathsf{fst}\!\left(\allowbreak\pthElm\allowbreak\right)}={\mathnormal{v_{I}}}
.
A
history
for player
\alpha\in\{{\mathtt{E}},{\mathtt{A}}\}
is a finite
initial path
\hstElm\in{\mathrm{Hst}_{\alpha}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Pth}}\cap({\mathrm{Ps}^{*}}\cdot{\mathrm{Ps}_{\alpha}})
terminating in an
\alpha
-position.
A
play

\playElm\in{\mathrm{Play}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Pth}}\cap{\mathrm{Ps}^{\omega}}

is just an infinite initial path.

A
strategy
for player
\alpha\in\{{\mathtt{E}},{\mathtt{A}}\}
is a function

\strElm[\alpha]\in{\mathrm{Str}_{\alpha}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Hst}_{\alpha}}\to{\mathrm{Ps}}

mapping each
\alpha
-history
\hstElm\in{\mathrm{Hst}_{\alpha}}
to a position

\strElm[\alpha](\hstElm)\in{\mathrm{Ps}}
compatible with the move relation,
i.e.
,

({\mathsf{lst}\!\left(\allowbreak\hstElm\allowbreak\right)},\allowbreak\strElm[\alpha](\hstElm))\in{\mathit{Mv}}
.
A path
\pthElm\in{\mathrm{Pth}}
is
compatible
with a pair of strategies

({\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}},{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}})\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\times{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}
if, for all
i\in{{[0\allowbreak,\allowbreak{{\lvert\allowbreak\pthElm\allowbreak\rvert}}-1)}}
, it holds that
(\pthElm)_{i+1}={\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}((\pthElm)_{\leq i})
, if
(\pthElm)_{i}\in{\mathrm{Ps_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}
, and

(\pthElm)_{i+1}={\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}((\pthElm)_{\leq i})
, otherwise.
The
play function

{\mathsf{play}}\colon{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\times{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}\to{\mathrm{Play}}
returns, for each pair of strategies
({\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}},{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}})\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\times{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}
, the unique play
{\mathsf{play}}({\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}},{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}})\in{\mathrm{Play}}
compatible with them.

The
observation function

{\mathsf{obs}}\colon{\mathrm{Pth}}\to{\mathrm{Ob}^{\infty}}

associates with each path
\pthElm\in{\mathrm{Pth}}
the ordered sequence
{\mathnormal{w}}\defeq{\mathsf{obs}}(\pthElm)\in{\mathrm{Ob}^{\infty}}
of all observable positions
occurring in it; formally, there exists a monotone bijection
{\mathsf{f}}\colon{{[0\allowbreak,\allowbreak{{\lvert\allowbreak{\mathnormal{w}}\allowbreak\rvert}})}}\!\to\!{{\{\,\allowbreak\!j\!\in\!{{[0\allowbreak,\allowbreak{{\lvert\allowbreak\pthElm\allowbreak\rvert}})}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak(\pthElm)_{j}\!\in\!{\mathrm{Ob}}\!\allowbreak\,\}}}
satisfying the equality
({\mathnormal{w}})_{i}=(\pthElm)_{{\mathsf{f}}(i)}
, for all
i\in{{[0\allowbreak,\allowbreak{{\lvert\allowbreak{\mathnormal{w}}\allowbreak\rvert}})}}
.

Eloise
wins
the game if there exists a strategy
{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}

such that
{\mathsf{obs}}({\mathsf{play}}({\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}},{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}))\in{\mathrm{Wn}}
, for all

{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}
.
Similarly, Abelard
wins
the game if there exists a strategy
{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}
such that
{\mathsf{obs}}({\mathsf{play}}({\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}},{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}))\in{{\overline{{\mathrm{Wn}}}}}
, for all
{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}
.

Theorem 0

(Quantification Game I)
.

For each behavioral quantification prefix
\qntElm\!\in\!{\mathrm{Qn}_{{\mathtt{B}}}}
and
Borelian property
\Psi\!\subseteq\!{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)})
, the game

{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\Psi}}
​ satisfies the following two properties:

1)

if Eloise wins then
{\mathrm{E}}\subseteq\Psi
, for some
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}({\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}(\qntElm))
;

2)

if Abelard wins then
{\mathrm{E}}\not\subseteq\Psi
, for all
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}({\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}(\qntElm))
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\Psi}}
be the game defined as prescribed in
Construction
1
.
Obviously, this is a Borelian game, due to the hypothesis on the property

\Psi
.

Before continuing, observe that, due to the specific structure of the game,
every history
\hstElm\cdot\posElm\in{\mathrm{Hst}_{\alpha}}
is bijectively
correlated with the sequence of positions
{\mathsf{obs}}(\hstElm)\cdot\posElm\in{\mathrm{Ob}}^{*}\cdot{\mathrm{Ps}_{\alpha}}
, for any player
\alpha\in\{{\mathtt{E}},{\mathtt{A}}\}
.
In other words, the functions
{\mathsf{\jmath}_{\alpha}}\colon{\mathrm{Hst}_{\alpha}}\to{\mathrm{Ob}}^{*}\cdot{\mathrm{Ps}_{\alpha}}
defined as
{\mathsf{\jmath}_{\alpha}}(\hstElm\cdot\posElm)\defeq{\mathsf{obs}}(\hstElm)\cdot\posElm
are bijective.

Thanks to this observation, it is thus immediate to show that, for each
strategy
{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}
, there is a unique function
{{\widehat{{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}}}}\colon{\mathrm{Ob}}^{*}\cdot{\mathrm{Ps_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\to{\mathrm{Ps}}
and,
vice versa
, for each
function
{{\widehat{{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}}}}\colon{\mathrm{Ob}}^{*}\cdot{\mathrm{Ps_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\to{\mathrm{Ps}}
, there
is a unique strategy
{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}
such that

{{\widehat{{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}}}}({\mathsf{\jmath}_{{\mathtt{E}}}}(\hstElm))={\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}(\hstElm),\text{ for
all histories }\hstElm\in{\mathrm{Hst_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}.

Similarly, for each strategy
{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}
, there is a unique
function
{{\widehat{{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}}}}\colon{\mathrm{Ob}}^{*}\cdot{\mathrm{Ps_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}\to{\mathrm{Ps}}
and,

vice versa
, for each function
{{\widehat{{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}}}}\colon{\mathrm{Ob}}^{*}\cdot{\mathrm{Ps_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}\to{\mathrm{Ps}}
, there is a unique strategy
{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}

satisfying the equality

{{\widehat{{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}}}}({\mathsf{\jmath}_{{\mathtt{A}}}}(\hstElm))={\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}(\hstElm),\text{ for
all histories }\hstElm\in{\mathrm{Hst_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}.

We can now proceed with the proof of the two properties.

•

[
]

Since Eloise wins the game, she has a winning strategy,
i.e.
, there is

{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}
such that
{\mathsf{obs}}({\mathsf{play}}({\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}},{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}))\in{\mathrm{Wn}}
, for all
{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}
.
We want to prove that there exists
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm))
such that

{\mathrm{E}}\subseteq\Psi
.

First, recall that
{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm)=\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}}\ldotp\exists^{{{\vec{\spcElm}}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}}
, for some
vectors of atomic propositions
{{\vec{\apElm}}},{{\vec{\qapElm}}}\in{\mathrm{AP}^{*}}
and quantifier specifications
{{\vec{\spcElm}}}\in{\mathrm{\mathbbo{\Theta}}}^{{{\lvert\allowbreak{{\vec{\qapElm}}}\allowbreak\rvert}}}
.
Moreover, thanks to Propositions
and
,
the following claim can be proved.

Claim 1
.

{\mathrm{E}}\subseteq\Psi
, for some
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm))
,
iff
there
exists
{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{{{\vec{\spcElm}}}}}({{\vec{\apElm}}})
such that

{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\asgElm,{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}},{{\vec{\qapElm}}}\allowbreak\right)}\in\Psi
, for all

\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({{\vec{\apElm}}})
.

Due to the above characterization of the existence of a set
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm))
such that
{\mathrm{E}}\subseteq\Psi
, the thesis can be proved by defining a suitable vector of
functors
{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{{{\vec{\spcElm}}}}}({{\vec{\apElm}}})
.

Consider an arbitrary assignment
\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({{\vec{\apElm}}})
and
define the function
{{\widehat{{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}}}^{\asgElm}}\colon{\mathrm{Ob}}^{*}\cdot{\mathrm{Ps_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}\to{\mathrm{Ps}}
as follows, for all finite sequences of observable
positions
\wrdElm\in{\mathrm{Ob}}^{*}
and Abelard’s positions
\valElm\in{\mathrm{Ps_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}
:

{{\widehat{{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}}}^{\asgElm}}(\wrdElm\cdot\valElm)\defeq\begin{cases}{{\varnothing}},&\text{if }\valElm\in{\mathrm{Ob}};\\
{\valElm}[\xapElm\mapsto\asgElm(\xapElm)({{\lvert\allowbreak\wrdElm\allowbreak\rvert}})],&\text{otherwise};\end{cases}

where
\xapElm\in{{\vec{\apElm}}}
is the atomic proposition at position

\#(\valElm)
in the prefix
\qntElm
,
i.e.
,
(\qntElm)_{\#(\valElm)}=\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}\xapElm
.
Due to the bijective correspondence previously described, there is a
unique strategy
{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}^{\asgElm}}\!\in\!{\mathrm{Str_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}
such that

{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}^{\asgElm}}(\hstElm)={{\widehat{{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}}}^{\asgElm}}({\mathsf{\jmath}_{{\mathtt{A}}}}(\hstElm))
, for all histories

\hstElm\in{\mathrm{Hst_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}
.
Obviously, the induced play
\playElm[][\asgElm]\defeq{\mathsf{play}}({\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}},{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}^{\asgElm}})
is won by Eloise,
i.e.
,
\wrdElm[][\asgElm]\defeq{\mathsf{obs}}(\playElm[][\asgElm])\in{\mathrm{Wn}}
.

Thanks to all the infinite sequences
\wrdElm[][\asgElm]
, one for each
assignment
\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({{\vec{\apElm}}})
, we can define every
component
({{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}})_{i}
of the vector of functors
{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}}\in({\mathrm{Fnc}}({{\vec{\apElm}}}))^{{{\lvert\allowbreak{{\vec{\qapElm}}}\allowbreak\rvert}}}
as follows, for all
instants of time
t\in{\mathbb{N}}
, where
i\in{{[0\allowbreak,\allowbreak{{\lvert\allowbreak{{\vec{\qapElm}}}\allowbreak\rvert}})}}
:

({{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}})_{i}(\asgElm)(t)\defeq\,(\wrdElm[][\asgElm])_{t}(({{\vec{\qapElm}}})_{i}).

It is not too hard to show that this functor complies with the vector of
quantifier specifications
{{\vec{\spcElm}}}
.

Claim 2
.

{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{{{\vec{\spcElm}}}}}({{\vec{\apElm}}})
.

At this point, for all assignments
\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({{\vec{\apElm}}})

\asgElm[{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}}]\defeq\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\asgElm,{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}},{{\vec{\qapElm}}}\allowbreak\right)}

We can easily argue that
\asgElm[{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}}]\in\Psi
.
Indeed, by construction of the strategy
{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}^{\asgElm}}
and the

{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}}

\asgElm[{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}}](\xapElm)(t)=(\wrdElm[][\asgElm])_{t}(\xapElm)

for all instants of time

and atomic propositions

Hence,
{\mathsf{wrd}}(\asgElm[{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}}])=\wrdElm[][\asgElm]
, which
implies
\asgElm[{{\vec{{\mathsf{F}}}}}]\in\Psi
, since
\wrdElm[][\asgElm]\in{\mathrm{Wn}}

[

Since Abelard wins the game, he has a winning strategy,

{\mathsf{obs}}({\mathsf{play}}({\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{E}}}}},{\mathnormal{\sigma_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}))\not\in{\mathrm{Wn}}

{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm)=\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}}\ldotp\forall^{{{\vec{\spcElm}}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}}

It is not too hard to show that this functor complies with the vector of

Claim 4

{{\vec{{\mathsf{G}}}}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{{{\vec{\spcElm}}}}}({{\vec{\qapElm}}})
