\asgElm[{{\vec{{\mathsf{G}}}}}]\not\in\Psi
, since
\wrdElm[][\asgElm]\not\in{\mathrm{Wn}}
.
∎

{\mathrm{\mathbbo{\Theta}}_{{\mathtt{B}}}}
is the set of behavioral quantifier specifications,
i.e.
,
quantifier specifications of the form
{\mathtt{B}}\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:{\mathrm{P}_{{\mathtt{S}}}}\right>
for some

{\mathrm{P}_{{\mathtt{S}}}}\subseteq{\mathrm{AP}}
.

Proposition 6
.

{\mathsf{evl}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}\apElm\ldotp\!\exists^{\spcElm\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:\apElm\right>}\qapElm)\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{evl}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},\exists^{\spcElm}\qapElm\ldotp\!\allowbreak\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}\apElm)
and
{\mathsf{evl}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}\apElm\ldotp\!\forall^{\spcElm\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:\apElm\right>}\qapElm)\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{evl}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},\forall^{\spcElm}\qapElm\ldotp\!\allowbreak\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}\apElm)
, for all
{\mathfrak{X}}\!\in\!{\mathrm{HAsg}}({\mathrm{P}})
with
{\mathrm{P}}\!\subseteq\!{\mathrm{AP}}
,
\apElm,\qapElm\in{\mathrm{AP}}\setminus{\mathrm{P}}
, and
\spcElm\in{\mathrm{\mathbbo{\Theta}}_{{\mathtt{B}}}}
.

Proof.

Due to the specific definition of the normal evaluation function

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)
, and by exploiting
Propositions
and
, the following claim can
be proved.

Claim 5
.

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}\apElm\ldotp\!\exists^{\spcElm\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:\apElm\right>}\qapElm)\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},\exists^{\spcElm}\qapElm\ldotp\!\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}\apElm)

iff
, for all
{\mathsf{J}}\!\in\!{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:\apElm\right>}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\cup\{\apElm\})
,
\eth\!\in\!{\mathrm{Fnc}_{{\mathtt{B}}}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})\!\to\!{\mathfrak{X}}
, and
{\mathsf{G}}\!\in\!{\mathrm{Fnc}_{{\mathtt{B}}}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\cup\{\qapElm\})
, there exists
{\mathsf{F}}\!\in\!{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})
and
{\mathrm{X}}\!\in\!{\mathfrak{X}}
such that

{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{F}},\qapElm\allowbreak\right)},{\mathsf{G}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\subseteq{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth,\apElm\allowbreak\right)},{\mathsf{J}},\qapElm\allowbreak\right)}
.

An analogous claim can be proved stating that the same characterization also
holds for
{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}\apElm\ldotp\!\forall^{\spcElm\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:\apElm\right>}\qapElm)\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},\forall^{\spcElm}\qapElm\ldotp\!\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}\apElm)
.
Thanks to such characterizations, the thesis can be shown by choosing a
suitable functor
{\mathsf{F}}
and set of assignments
{\mathrm{X}}
in dependence of the
functors
{\mathsf{J}}
and
{\mathsf{G}}
and the selection map
\eth
.

In order to define the functor
{\mathsf{F}}
, let us inductively construct, for
every given assignment
\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})
, the following
infinite families of assignments
\{\aasgElm[t][\asgElm]\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\cup\{\apElm\})\}_{t\in{\mathbb{N}}}
, Boolean values
\{{\mathnormal{v}_{t}^{\asgElm}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{B}}\}_{t\in{\mathbb{N}}}
, and assignments
\{\basgElm[t][\asgElm]\!\in\!{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\cup\{\qapElm\})\}_{t\in{\mathbb{N}}}
:

•

as base step
t\!=\!0
, we choose
\aasgElm[0][\asgElm]\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\cup\{\apElm\})
as an arbitrary assignment for
which the equality
\aasgElm[0][\asgElm]\rst{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}=\asgElm

holds true, the Boolean value
{\mathnormal{v}_{0}^{\asgElm}}\in{\mathbb{B}}
as

{\mathsf{J}}(\aasgElm[0][\asgElm])(0)
,
i.e.
,
{\mathnormal{v}_{0}^{\asgElm}}\defeq{\mathsf{J}}(\aasgElm[0][\asgElm])(0)
, and
\basgElm[0][\asgElm]\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\cup\{\qapElm\})
as an arbitrary assignment with

\basgElm[0][\asgElm]\rst{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}=\asgElm
such that, at time
0

on the variable
\qapElm
, assumes
{\mathnormal{v}_{0}^{\asgElm}}
as value,
i.e.
,

\basgElm[0][\asgElm](\qapElm)(0)={\mathnormal{v}_{0}^{\asgElm}}
;

•

as inductive step
t>0
, we derive the assignment
\aasgElm[t][\asgElm]\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\cup\{\apElm\})
from
{\mathsf{G}}(\basgElm[t-1][\asgElm])
,
i.e.
,
\aasgElm[t][\asgElm]\defeq{\asgElm}[\apElm\mapsto{\mathsf{G}}(\basgElm[t-1][\asgElm])]
, and the Boolean value

{\mathnormal{v}_{t}^{\asgElm}}\in{\mathbb{B}}
from
{\mathsf{J}}(\aasgElm[t][\asgElm])(t)
,
i.e.
,

{\mathnormal{v}_{t}^{\asgElm}}\defeq{\mathsf{J}}(\aasgElm[t][\asgElm])(t)
; moreover, we
choose
\basgElm[t][\asgElm]\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\cup\{\qapElm\})

as an arbitrary assignment with
\basgElm[t][\asgElm]\rst{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}=\asgElm
such that, on the variable
\qapElm
, is equal to
\basgElm[t-1][\asgElm]
up to time
t
excluded and assumes
{\mathnormal{v}_{t}^{\asgElm}}
as
value at time
t
,
i.e.
,
\basgElm[t][\asgElm](\qapElm)(h)=\basgElm[t-1][\asgElm](\qapElm)(h)
, for all
h\in{{[0\allowbreak,\allowbreak t)}}
, and

\basgElm[t][\asgElm](\qapElm)(t)={\mathnormal{v}_{t}^{\asgElm}}
.

Thanks to the infinite family of Boolean values
\{{\mathnormal{v}_{t}^{\asgElm}}\in{\mathbb{B}}\}_{t\in{\mathbb{N}}}
, one for each assignment
\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})
, we can define the functor
{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})
as follows, for every instant of time
t\in{\mathbb{N}}
:

{\mathsf{F}}(\asgElm)(t)\defeq{\mathnormal{v}_{t}^{\asgElm}}.

It is easy to show that this functor complies with the quantifier
specification
\spcElm
, since the functor
{\mathsf{J}}
, from which
{\mathsf{F}}
is
derived, is compliant with the quantifier specification
\spcElm\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:\apElm\right>
.

Claim 6
.

{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})
.

Before continuing, let us first introduce the functor
{\mathsf{H}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})
as follows, for every assignment
\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})
:

{\mathsf{H}}(\asgElm)\defeq{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\asgElm,{\mathsf{F}},\qapElm\allowbreak\right)},{\mathsf{G}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}(\apElm).

It is quite immediate to verify that such a functor is behavioral.

Claim 7
.

{\mathsf{H}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{{\mathtt{B}}}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)})
.

At this point, consider the set of assignments
{\mathrm{X}}\defeq\eth({\mathsf{H}})
.
Thanks to the specific definitions of the two functors
{\mathsf{F}}
and
{\mathsf{H}}
,
the following claim can be proved.

Claim 8
.

{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{F}},\qapElm\allowbreak\right)},{\mathsf{G}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\subseteq{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{H}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},{\mathsf{J}},\qapElm\allowbreak\right)}
.

Now, it is obvious that
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{H}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\subseteq{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth,\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
, due to the definition of the latter and the choice of the set

{\mathrm{X}}
, which immediately implies
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{H}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},{\mathsf{J}},\qapElm\allowbreak\right)}\subseteq{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth,\apElm\allowbreak\right)},{\mathsf{J}},\qapElm\allowbreak\right)}
.
Therefore,
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}},{\mathsf{F}},\qapElm\allowbreak\right)},{\mathsf{G}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\subseteq{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth,\apElm\allowbreak\right)},{\mathsf{J}},\qapElm\allowbreak\right)}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 0
.

\!{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm))\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm))
, for
{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}
and
\qntElm\in{\mathrm{Qn}_{{\mathtt{B}}}}
, with
{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}\cap{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

First, we notice that, by repeatedly applying Proposition
,
we are able to derive the following claim.

Claim 9
.

{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}}\ldotp\!\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:{{\vec{\apElm}}}\right>}{{\vec{\qapElm}}})\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}}\ldotp\!\allowbreak\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}})
and

{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}}\ldotp\!\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:{{\vec{\apElm}}}\right>}{{\vec{\qapElm}}})\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}}\ldotp\!\allowbreak\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}})
, for all
{\mathfrak{X}}\!\in\!{\mathrm{HAsg}}({\mathrm{P}})
with
{\mathrm{P}}\!\subseteq\!{\mathrm{AP}}
and
{{\vec{\apElm}}},{{\vec{\qapElm}}}\subseteq{\mathrm{AP}}\setminus{\mathrm{P}}
.

Let us first focus on proving
\!{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm))\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)
and consider the case
\alpha=\exists\forall
.
First, let us rewrite
\qntElm
as
\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{0}}\ldotp(\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{i}}\ldotp\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{i}})_{i=1}^{k}\ldotp\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{k+1}}
, where
|{{\vec{\apElm}}_{0}}|,|{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{k+1}}|\geq 0
,
k\geq 0
, and
|{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{i}}|,|{{\vec{\apElm}}_{i}}|\geq 1
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
. The proof proceeds by induction on the

\exists\forall
-alternation degree
of
\qntElm
, that is,
k
.

If
k=0
(base case), then
{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm)=\qntElm
, and we
are done.
If
k>0
(inductive step), then
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)={\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}(\allowbreak{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{0}}\ldotp(\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{i}}\ldotp\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{i}})_{i=1}^{k-1}),\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{k}}\ldotp\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{k}}),\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{k+1}})
.
Thanks to the above claim and to the monotonicity of
\mathsf{nevl}
, we have

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)\sqsupseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}(\allowbreak{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}(\allowbreak{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{0}}\ldotp(\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{i}}\ldotp\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{i}})_{i=1}^{k-1}),\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{k}}\ldotp\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:{{\vec{\apElm}}_{k}}\right>}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{k}}),\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{k+1}})={\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}(\allowbreak{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm^{\prime}),\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:{{\vec{\apElm}}_{k}}\right>}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{k}}\ldotp\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{k+1}})
, where
\qntElm^{\prime}=\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{0}}\ldotp(\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{i}}\ldotp\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{i}})_{i=1}^{k-2}\ldotp\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{k-1}}\ldotp\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{k-1}}\ldotp\forall^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\apElm}}_{k}}
has

\exists\forall
-alternation degree
k-1
.
Thus, by inductive hypothesis, it holds that

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm^{\prime}))\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm^{\prime})
.
The thesis follows by observing that
{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm^{\prime})\ldotp\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}\cup\left<{\mathtt{S}}:{{\vec{\apElm}}_{k}}\right>}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{k}}\ldotp\exists^{{\mathtt{B}}}{{\vec{\qapElm}}_{k+1}}={\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm)
.

In order to prove that
\!{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm))\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)
holds when
\alpha={\mathtt{\forall\exists}}
, it suffices to replace quantifier

\forall
with
\exists
, and vice versa, inside the quantifier prefixes, and to
switch
{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}
and
{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}
throughout the previous proof. We omit the details.

Finally, in order to prove that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm))
for every

\alpha\in\{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}},{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}\}
, we first state two auxiliary results.

Claim 10
.

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},{{\overline{\qntElm}}})\equiv{{\overline{{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)}}}
, for all
{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}
and

\qntElm\in{\mathrm{Qn}}
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the length of
\qntElm
.
If
\qntElm=\varepsilon
, then the claim follows immediately.
Let
\qntElm={\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm^{\prime}
, then we distinguish
two cases.

If
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are coherent, then
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},{{\overline{\qntElm}}})\equiv{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},{{\overline{\qntElm^{\prime}}}})
(recall that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)\equiv{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
due to
Proposition
).
By
Propositions
,
,
,
and
, we have that

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},{{\overline{\qntElm^{\prime}}}})\equiv{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},{{\overline{\qntElm^{\prime}}}})
.
Moreover, we have that
{{\overline{{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)}}}={{\overline{{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},\qntElm^{\prime})}}}
.
By inductive hypothesis, it holds that

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},{{\overline{\qntElm^{\prime}}}})\equiv{{\overline{{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},\qntElm^{\prime})}}}
,
hence the thesis.

If
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are not coherent, then

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},{{\overline{\qntElm}}})={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},{{\overline{\qntElm^{\prime}}}})
.
Moreover,
{{\overline{{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)}}}\equiv{{\overline{{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},\qntElm^{\prime})}}}
.
By inductive hypothesis,

{{\overline{{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}},\qntElm^{\prime})}}}\equiv{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{{\overline{{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}}}}}}},{{\overline{\qntElm^{\prime}}}})\equiv{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},{{\overline{\qntElm^{\prime}}}})={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},{{\overline{\qntElm}}})

∎

It is not difficult to convince oneself that the following claim holds.

Claim 11
.

{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}({{\overline{\qntElm}}})={{\overline{{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm)}}}
for
all
\qntElm\in{\mathrm{Qn}}
.

Now, by instantiating
{\mathfrak{X}}
and
\qntElm
with
{{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}}
and

{{\overline{\qntElm}}}
, respectively, in the first “squared inclusion” of this
proposition, proved in the first part of this proof, we know that

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}({{\overline{\qntElm}}}))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}},{{\overline{\qntElm}}})
.
By Claims
10
and
11
, we have

{{\overline{{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm))}}}\sqsubseteq{{\overline{{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)}}}
.
The thesis follows from Proposition
.
∎

Proposition 6
.

\!{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]))\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathscr{Y}}\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2])
, for all
{\mathscr{Y}}\in\{{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[2])),{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])\}
,
{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}
and
\qntElm[1],\qntElm[2]\in{\mathrm{Qn}_{{\mathtt{B}}}}
,
with
{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]\allowbreak\right)}\cap{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

We will make use of the following result.

Claim 12
.

{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}({\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])={\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2])={\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[2]))
for all
\qntElm[1],\qntElm[2]\in{\mathrm{Qn}}
.

Remark 1
.

Notice that due to the fact that
{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1])\notin{\mathrm{Qn}_{{\mathtt{B}}}}
and
{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[2])\notin{\mathrm{Qn}_{{\mathtt{B}}}}
, the two
expressions
{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}({\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])
and

{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[2]))
are not well
defined.
Thus, in order for the above claim to make sense, we need generalized
definitions for functions
{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}
and
{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}
to cope with
quantifier prefixes featuring arbitrary quantifier specifications.

1.

In order to show that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[2]))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2])
, we proceed as follows:

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2])={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]),\qntElm[2])\sqsupseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]),{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[2]))={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[2]))
.

2.

In order to show that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2])
, we proceed as follows:

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2])={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]),\qntElm[2])\sqsupseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1])),\qntElm[2])={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])
.

3.

In order to show that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[2]))
,
we proceed as follows:
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]))={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[2])))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[2]))
.

4.

In order to show that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])
,
we proceed as follows:
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]))={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}({\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2]))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{{\overline{\alpha}}}}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])
.∎

Proposition 7
.

\!{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2])\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathscr{Y}}\!\sqsubseteq\!{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]))
, for all
{\mathscr{Y}}\in\{{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[2])),{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])\}
,
{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}
and

\qntElm[1],\qntElm[2]\in{\mathrm{Qn}_{{\mathtt{B}}}}
, with
{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]\allowbreak\right)}\cap{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

1.

In order to show that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2])\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[2]))
, we proceed as follows:
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2])={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]),\qntElm[2])\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]),{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[2]))={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[2]))
.

2.

In order to show that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2])\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])
, we proceed as follows:

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2])={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]),\qntElm[2])\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1])),\qntElm[2])={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])
.

3.

In order to show that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[2]))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]))
, we proceed as follows:
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[2]))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[2])))={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]))
.

4.

In order to show that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]))
, we
proceed as follows:
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2])\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}({\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1])\ldotp\qntElm[2]))={\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\alpha}}(\qntElm[1]\ldotp\qntElm[2]))
.∎

Theorem 0

(Quantification Game II)
.

Every
{\mathfrak{Q}}
-game
{\mathcal{G}}
, for some quantification-game
schema
{\mathfrak{Q}}\defeq{{\left\langle\qntElm\allowbreak,\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak,\allowbreak\Psi\right\rangle}}
, satisfies the
following two properties:

1)

if Eloise wins then
{\mathrm{E}}\subseteq\Psi
, for some
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}(\qntElm))
;

2)

if Abelard wins then
{\mathrm{E}}\not\subseteq\Psi
, for all
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}(\qntElm))
.

Proof.

First of all, recall that the game
{\mathcal{G}_{{\mathfrak{Q}}}}
of
Construction
2
is obtained from the game

{\mathcal{G}_{{{\widehat{\qntElm}}}}^{{{\widehat{\Psi}}}}}
given in Construction
1
,
where

•

{{\widehat{\qntElm}}}\defeq\forall{{\vec{\apElm}}}\ldotp\qntElm[][\bullet]\ldotp\qntElm
and

•

{{\widehat{\Psi}}}\defeq\Psi\cup{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathrm{P}})\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{{\vec{\apElm}}}}\,\not\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}
,

with
{{\vec{\apElm}}}\defeq{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\setminus{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm[][\bullet]\allowbreak\right)}
and

{\mathrm{P}}\defeq{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}\cup{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}
and
being
{{\left\langle\qntElm[][\bullet]\allowbreak,\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}\right\rangle}}
a generator for

{\mathfrak{X}}
.

We can now proceed with the proof of the two properties.

•

[
]

If Eloise wins the game, by Theorem
, there exists a set
of assignments
{{\widehat{{\mathrm{E}}}}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}({{\widehat{\qntElm}}}))
such that

{{\widehat{{\mathrm{E}}}}}\subseteq{{\widehat{\Psi}}}
.
Thanks to Propositions
and
, we can
easily prove the following inclusion between normal evaluations.

Claim 13
.

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}({{\widehat{\qntElm}}}))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm))
.

Due to the specific definition of the ordering
\sqsubseteq
between
hyperassignments, it follows that the above inclusion necessarily implies
the existence of a set of assignments
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm))
such
that
{\mathrm{E}}\!\subseteq\!{{\widehat{{\mathrm{E}}}}}
.
Therefore,
{\mathrm{E}}\!\subseteq\!{{\widehat{\Psi}}}
.

At this point, we can immediately prove that
{\mathrm{E}}\subseteq\Psi
, being

{{\widehat{\Psi}}}=\Psi\cup{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathrm{P}})\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{{\vec{\apElm}}}}\,\not\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}
, thanks to the following claim, which
can be derived from Proposition
.

Claim 14
.

{\mathrm{E}}\cap{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathrm{P}})\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{{\vec{\apElm}}}}\,\not\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}=\emptyset
, for all
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm))
.

•

[
]

If Abelard wins the game, by Theorem
, for all sets of
assignments
{{\widehat{{\mathrm{E}}}}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}({{\widehat{\qntElm}}}))
it holds
that
{{\widehat{{\mathrm{E}}}}}\not\subseteq{{\widehat{\Psi}}}
.

First, given
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}
,
with
{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)}={\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\allowbreak\right)}
, and
{\mathrm{X}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathrm{P}})
for some
{\mathrm{P}}\subseteq{\mathrm{AP}}
, we define
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\sqsubseteq_{{\mathrm{X}}}{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
if and only if for every
{\mathrm{X}_{1}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}
there is
{\mathrm{X}_{2}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
such that
{\mathrm{X}_{2}}\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{\mathrm{P}}}\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}\subseteq{\mathrm{X}_{1}}
.
The following claim holds.

Claim 15
.

Let
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}
and
{\mathrm{X}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathrm{P}})
for some
{\mathrm{P}}\subseteq{\mathrm{AP}}
.
Then,
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\sqsubseteq_{{\mathrm{X}}}{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
implies

{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\qntElm)\sqsubseteq_{{\mathrm{X}}}{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\qntElm)
, for all
\qntElm\in{\mathrm{Qn}}

with
{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)}\cap{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the length of
\qntElm
.

If
\qntElm=\varepsilon
(base case), then the claim follows
trivially.

Let
\qntElm={\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm\ldotp\qntElm^{\prime}
.
If
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are coherent, then
{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{i}},\qntElm)={\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{i}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)},\qntElm^{\prime})
, for
every
i\in\{1,2\}
.
We show that
{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\sqsubseteq_{{\mathrm{X}}}{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
holds, and the thesis follows by applying
the inductive hypothesis.
Since
{\mathsf{ext}_{\spcElm}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{i}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}={{\{\,\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}_{i}},{\mathsf{F}_{i}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}_{i}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathsf{F}}_{i}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{i}}\allowbreak\right)})\allowbreak\,\}}}
(
i\in\{1,2\}
), we have to show that
for every
{\mathrm{X}_{1}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}
and
{\mathsf{F}_{1}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})
(
={\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\allowbreak\right)})
) there
are
{\mathrm{X}_{2}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
and
{\mathsf{F}_{2}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})
such that
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}_{2}},{\mathsf{F}_{2}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{\mathrm{P}}}\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}\subseteq{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}_{1}},{\mathsf{F}_{1}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
.
It is easy to see that for every
{\mathrm{X}_{1}}\in{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}
and
{\mathsf{F}_{1}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})
, it holds that
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak f({\mathrm{X}_{1}}),{\mathsf{F}_{1}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{\mathrm{P}}}\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}\subseteq{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}_{1}},{\mathsf{F}_{1}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
, where
f:{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
is a witness for
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\sqsubseteq_{{\mathrm{X}}}{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
.

Instead, if
\alpha
and
{\mathtt{Q}}
are not coherent, then

{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{i}},\qntElm)\equiv{\mathsf{evl}_{\alpha}}({\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{i}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm),\qntElm^{\prime})
,
for every
i\in\{1,2\}
.
We show that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{1}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)\sqsubseteq_{{\mathrm{X}}}{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{2}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)
holds,
and the thesis follows by applying the inductive hypothesis.
Since
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}_{i}},{\mathtt{Q}}^{\spcElm}\apElm)={{\{\,\allowbreak{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth_{i},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\eth_{i}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{i}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}_{i}}\allowbreak\,\}}}
, where
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth_{i},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}=\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathsf{dom}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth_{i}\allowbreak\right)}}{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth_{i}({\mathsf{F}}),{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
(
i\in\{1,2\}
), we have to show that
for every
\eth_{1}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}
there is

\eth_{2}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
(recall that

{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)}={\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}\allowbreak\right)}
) such that
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth_{2},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{\mathrm{P}}}\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}\subseteq{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth_{1},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
.
To this end, we define a function
g:({\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}_{1}})\rightarrow({\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}_{2}})

as follows.
For every
\eth_{1}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}

and
{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})
, we define
g(\eth_{1})({\mathsf{F}})=f(\eth_{1}({\mathsf{F}}))
.
Clearly, since
f
is a witness for
{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\sqsubseteq_{{\mathrm{X}}}{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}
, it holds that
g(\eth_{1})({\mathsf{F}})\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{\mathrm{P}}}\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}\subseteq\eth_{1}({\mathsf{F}})
, for every
{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})
.
Thus, for every
\eth_{1}\in{\mathrm{Fnc}_{\spcElm}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}\allowbreak\right)})\to{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}
, the following holds:
{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak g(\eth_{1}),\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{\mathrm{P}}}\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}=\left(\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathsf{dom}\!\left(\allowbreak g(\eth_{1})\allowbreak\right)}}{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak g(\eth_{1})({\mathsf{F}}),{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\right)\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{\mathrm{P}}}\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}=\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathsf{dom}\!\left(\allowbreak g(\eth_{1})\allowbreak\right)}}({\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak g(\eth_{1})({\mathsf{F}}),{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{\mathrm{P}}}\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}})\subseteq\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathsf{dom}\!\left(\allowbreak g(\eth_{1})\allowbreak\right)}}{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak g(\eth_{1})({\mathsf{F}})\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{\mathrm{P}}}\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}},{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}

\subseteq\bigcup_{{\mathsf{F}}\in{\mathsf{dom}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth_{1}\allowbreak\right)}}{\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth_{1}({\mathsf{F}}),{\mathsf{F}},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}={\mathsf{ext}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth_{1},\apElm\allowbreak\right)}
(notice that
{\mathsf{dom}\!\left(\allowbreak g(\eth_{1})\allowbreak\right)}={\mathsf{dom}\!\left(\allowbreak\eth_{1}\allowbreak\right)}
).
∎

Now, notice that, by definition of generator,
{\mathrm{X}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Asg}}({{\vec{\apElm}}})
.
Let
{{\overline{{\mathrm{X}}}}}\defeq{\mathrm{Asg}}({{\vec{\apElm}}})\setminus{\mathrm{X}}
.
Thanks to Propositions
,
it is possible to prove the following claim, by observing that, clearly,

\{{\mathrm{X}}\}\sqsubseteq_{{{\overline{{\mathrm{X}}}}}}\{{\mathrm{Asg}}({{\vec{\apElm}}})\}
holds, as

{\mathrm{Asg}}({{\vec{\apElm}}})\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{{\vec{\apElm}}}}\in{{\overline{{\mathrm{X}}}}}\allowbreak\,\}}}={\mathrm{X}}
.

Claim 16
.

{\mathsf{evl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm))\sqsubseteq_{{{\overline{{\mathrm{X}}}}}}{\mathsf{evl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}({{\widehat{\qntElm}}}))
.

Due to the specific definition of the ordering
\sqsubseteq
between
hyperassignments, it follows that the above inclusion necessarily implies
the non existence of a set of assignments
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm))
such
that
{\mathrm{E}}\subseteq{{\widehat{\Psi}}}
.
Indeed, assume, towards a contradiction that there is
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm))
such that

{\mathrm{E}}\subseteq{{\widehat{\Psi}}}
.
By the above claim, there is
{{\widehat{{\mathrm{E}}}}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}({{\widehat{\qntElm}}}))
such that

{{\widehat{{\mathrm{E}}}}}\setminus{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{{\vec{\apElm}}}}\in{{\overline{{\mathrm{X}}}}}\allowbreak\,\}}}\subseteq{\mathrm{E}}\subseteq{{\widehat{\Psi}}}
.
Since
{{\widehat{{\mathrm{E}}}}}\cap{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{{\vec{\apElm}}}}\in{{\overline{{\mathrm{X}}}}}\allowbreak\,\}}}\subseteq{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathrm{P}})\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{{\vec{\apElm}}}}\,\not\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}\subseteq{{\widehat{\Psi}}}
, we have
that
{{\widehat{{\mathrm{E}}}}}\subseteq{{\widehat{\Psi}}}
, which is in contradiction with Abelard
winning the game.
Hence,
{\mathrm{E}}\not\subseteq{{\widehat{\Psi}}}
holds for all
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm))
, which implies

{\mathrm{E}}\not\subseteq\Psi
, for all
{\mathrm{E}}\in{\mathsf{evl}_{\exists\forall}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm))
, being
\Psi\subseteq{{\widehat{\Psi}}}
.
∎

Theorem 0

(Formula Canonical Forms)
.

For every behavioral
GFG
-
Q
PTL
formula
\qntElm\psi
, with
\psi\in\text{LTL$$}
,
it holds that
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\qntElm\psi

iff

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}(\qntElm)\psi

iff

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}(\qntElm)\psi
, for all Borelian behavioral hyperassignments

{\mathfrak{X}}\in{\mathrm{HAsg}}({\mathsf{free}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\psi\allowbreak\right)})
.

Proof.

By Proposition
and Lemma
, we have that

{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\qntElm\psi

iff

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm){{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\psi
,
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm)\psi

iff

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm)){{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\psi
, and
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm)\psi

iff

{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm)){{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\psi
.

If
\alpha={\mathtt{\exists\forall}}
, then, by Proposition
, it holds
that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm))
.
Therefore, by Theorem
, we have that
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm)\psi
implies
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\qntElm\psi
, which, in turn, implies
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm)\psi
.

If
\alpha={\mathtt{\forall\exists}}
, then, by Proposition
, it holds
that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},\qntElm)\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{\alpha}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm))
.
Therefore, by Theorem
, we have that
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm)\psi
implies
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}\qntElm\psi
, which, in turn, implies
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{\alpha}}{\mathtt{C}_{\forall\exists}}(\qntElm)\psi
.

We are now left to show that
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}(\qntElm)\psi
implies
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}(\qntElm)\psi
and that
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}(\qntElm)\psi
implies
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}(\qntElm)\psi
.

In order to prove the former, we proceed as follows.
Let
\Psi\defeq{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{free}\!\left(\allowbreak\psi\allowbreak\right)})\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm{{\,\models}_{\text{LTL$$}}}\psi\allowbreak\,\}}}
and notice that
\Psi
is Borelian
[
54
]
.
Observe that
{\mathfrak{Q}}={{\left\langle{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak,\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak,\allowbreak\Psi\right\rangle}}
is a quantification-game schema.
Since
{\mathfrak{X}}
is Borelian behavioral, then there is a generator
{{\left\langle\tilde{\qntElm}\allowbreak,\allowbreak{\mathrm{X}}\right\rangle}}
for it.
Therefore, the game
{\mathcal{G}_{{{\widehat{\qntElm}}}}^{{{\widehat{\Psi}}}}}
is a
{\mathfrak{Q}}
-game,
where
{{\vec{p}}}\defeq{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak{\mathfrak{X}}\allowbreak\right)}\setminus{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\tilde{\qntElm}\allowbreak\right)}
,

{{\widehat{\qntElm}}}\defeq\forall{{\vec{\apElm}}}\ldotp\tilde{\qntElm}\ldotp\qntElm
, and
{{\widehat{\Psi}}}\defeq\Psi\cup{{\{\,\allowbreak\asgElm\in{\mathrm{Asg}}({\mathsf{free}\!\left(\allowbreak\psi\allowbreak\right)})\allowbreak\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak\allowbreak\asgElm\rst{{{\vec{\apElm}}}}\,\not\in{\mathrm{X}}\allowbreak\,\}}}
.
By Proposition
and Item
of
Theorem
,
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}{\mathtt{C}_{\exists\forall}}(\qntElm)\psi
implies that there exists
{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}(\qntElm))
such that
{\mathrm{X}}\subseteq\Psi
.
By Item
of Theorem
, it holds that
Abelard does not win the game
{\mathcal{G}_{{{\widehat{\qntElm}}}}^{{{\widehat{\Psi}}}}}
.
Now, notice that
{\mathcal{G}_{{{\widehat{\qntElm}}}}^{{{\widehat{\Psi}}}}}
is Borelian; thus, as a
consequence of Martin’s determinacy theorem
[
46
,
47
]
,

{\mathcal{G}_{{{\widehat{\qntElm}}}}^{{{\widehat{\Psi}}}}}
is won by Eloise.
Hence, from Item
of Theorem
, it
follows that there exists
{\mathrm{X}}\in{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}(\qntElm))
such that
\asgElm{{\,\models}_{\text{LTL$$}}}\psi
for all
\asgElm\in{\mathrm{X}}
, which, by Proposition
and
Item
of Theorem
, implies
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}(\qntElm)\psi
.

Finally, we turn to showing that
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}(\qntElm)\psi
implies
{\mathfrak{X}}{{\,\models}^{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}(\qntElm)\psi
.
By Proposition
, it holds that

{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}(\qntElm))\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}(\qntElm))
.
By Theorem
, we have that
{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}(\qntElm)){{\,\models}^{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}\psi
implies
{\mathsf{nevl}_{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}({\mathfrak{X}},{\mathtt{C}_{{\mathtt{\exists\forall}}}}(\qntElm)){{\,\models}^{{\mathtt{\forall\exists}}}}\psi
.
The thesis follows from Lemma
and
Proposition
.
∎

-D

Proofs for Section
V

Theorem 0

(Satisfiability Game)
.

For every behavioral
GFG
-
Q
PTL
sentence
\varphi
there is a parity game,
with
{\mathrm{O}\!\left(\allowbreak 2^{2^{{{\lvert\allowbreak\varphi\allowbreak\rvert}}}}\allowbreak\right)}
positions and

{\mathrm{O}\!\left(\allowbreak 2^{{{\lvert\allowbreak\varphi\allowbreak\rvert}}}\allowbreak\right)}
priorities, won by Eloise
iff

\varphi
is
satisfiable.

Proof.

Let
\varphi=\qntElm\psi
be a behavioral
Q
PTL
prenex sentence with
\qntElm
a quantification prefix and
\psi

an
LTL
formula. The idea of the proof is to construct a parity game from the
automaton recognizing models of
\psi
and the quantification prefix that
will be equivalent to the game
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
defined in
Construction
1
.

From
\psi
, we construct a non-deterministic Büchi automaton

A_{\psi}
recognizing models of
\psi
using the
Vardi-Wolper construction
[
66
]
. We will consider a complete determinized
parity Automaton
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}={{\left\langle Q\allowbreak,\allowbreak q_{0}\allowbreak,\allowbreak\Sigma\allowbreak,\allowbreak\delta\allowbreak,\allowbreak\text{Acc}\right\rangle}}
equivalent to
A_{\psi}
(that can be
obtained via a Safra-like determinization procedure
[
55
]
) with

•

Q
is the set of states of
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
,

•

q_{0}\in Q
is the initial state,

•

\Sigma={\mathrm{Val}}
is the alphabet of
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
,

•

\delta:Q\times\Sigma\rightarrow Q
is the transition function,

•

Acc
is the parity condition.

The parity game associated is defined as
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}\defeq{{\left\langle{\mathcal{A}}\allowbreak,\allowbreak{\mathrm{Ob}}\allowbreak,\allowbreak{\mathrm{Wn}}\right\rangle}}
with arena
{\mathcal{A}}\defeq{{\left\langle{\mathrm{Ps_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\allowbreak,\allowbreak{\mathrm{Ps_{{\mathtt{A}}}}}\allowbreak,\allowbreak{\mathnormal{v_{I}}}\allowbreak,\allowbreak{\mathit{Mv}}\right\rangle}}
and is constructed as follows:

•

the set of positions
{\mathrm{Ps}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})\subseteq Q\times{\mathrm{Ps}}({\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}})
contains exactly the pairs of one state of the automaton
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
and a valuation
\valElm\in{\mathrm{Val}}
that is a position of
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
;

•

the set of Eloise’s positions
{\mathrm{Ps_{{\mathtt{E}}}}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Ps}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})
only contains
the positions
(q,\valElm)\in{\mathrm{Ps}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})
for
\valElm
is an Eloise’s position in
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
;

•

the initial position
{\mathnormal{v_{I}}}\defeq(q_{0},{{\varnothing}})
is just the initial state of
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
paired with the initial state of
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
;

•

the move relation
{\mathit{Mv}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Ps}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})\times{\mathrm{Ps}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})
contains
exactly those pairs of positions
((q_{1},\valElm[1]),(q_{2},\valElm[2]))\in{\mathrm{Ps}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})\times{\mathrm{Ps}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})
such that:

–

(\valElm[1],\valElm[2])
is a move in
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
;

–

if
\valElm[2]={{\varnothing}}
then
q_{2}=\delta(q_{1},\valElm[1])
, otherwise,
q_{1}=q_{2}
;

•

the set of observable positions
{\mathrm{Ob}}\defeq Q\times{{\{\,\allowbreak{{\varnothing}}\allowbreak\allowbreak\,\}}}
;

•

the winning condition is deduced from the accepting condition of the automaton
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
. More precisely, the priority of a position
(q,{{\varnothing}})
is defined as the priority of
q
.

We first show that the arena of the game
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
is very similar to the arena of
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
trough the definition of a bijection between initial paths on
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
(denoted
{\mathrm{Pth}}_{\text{init}}({\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}})
) and initial paths on
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
(denoted
{\mathrm{Pth}}_{\text{init}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})
)
f:{\mathrm{Pth}}_{\text{init}}({\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}})\rightarrow{\mathrm{Pth}}_{\text{init}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})
.

We consider the morphism
\mathtt{f}\colon{\mathrm{Ps}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})\to{\mathrm{Ps}}({\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}})
defined on
(q,\valElm)
by the following.

\mathtt{f}((q,\valElm))\defeq\valElm

We now define
f
on
\pthElm\in{\mathrm{Pth}}_{\text{init}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})
by the following.

f(\pthElm)\defeq\mathtt{f}(\pthElm)

Claim 17
.

The function
f
between
{\mathrm{Pth}}_{\text{init}}({\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}})
and
{\mathrm{Pth}}_{\text{init}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})
is a bijection.

Proof.

First, we show that
\mathtt{f}
is indeed a morphism:

•

there is a move
(q,\valElm_{1})(q,\valElm_{2})
for some
q\in Q
in
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}

iff
there is a move
\valElm_{1}\valElm_{2}
in
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
,

•

there is a move
(q,\valElm)(q^{\prime},{{\varnothing}})
for some
\valElm\in{\mathrm{Val}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)})
in
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}

iff
there is a move
\valElm{{\varnothing}}
in
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
.

We conclude that if
(q_{1},\valElm_{1})(q_{2},\valElm_{2})
is a move in
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
, then
\mathtt{f}((q_{1},\valElm_{1})(q_{2},\valElm_{2}))
is a move in
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
. Then
f
is well defined.

The inverse function is also well defined because we consider only initial paths and the automaton
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
is deterministic and complete. Thus, from an history
{\hstElm}
of
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
, there is only one state
q
reached by
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
by reading
{\mathsf{obs}}({\hstElm})
. Formally
f^{-1}
can be defined inductively on a
\alpha
-history
{\hstElm}={\hstElm}^{\prime}\valElm
as follows:

•

f^{-1}({{\varnothing}})=(q_{0},{{\varnothing}}^{\prime})

•

if
{\mathsf{lst}\!\left(\allowbreak f^{-1}({\hstElm}^{\prime}\allowbreak\right)})=(q,\valElm)
with
{\mathsf{dom}\!\left(\allowbreak\valElm\allowbreak\right)}={\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak(\qntElm)_{<\#(\valElm)}\allowbreak\right)}
, then
f^{-1}({\hstElm})=f^{-1}({\hstElm}^{\prime})(\delta(q,\valElm),{{\varnothing}})
,

•

otherwise,
{\mathsf{lst}\!\left(\allowbreak f^{-1}({\hstElm}^{\prime})\allowbreak\right)}=(q,\valElm^{\prime})
for some
q\in Q
thus
f^{-1}({\hstElm})=f^{-1}({\hstElm}^{\prime})(q,\valElm)
.

with
{\mathsf{lst}\!\left(\allowbreak\wrdElm\allowbreak\right)}
being the last character of the finite word
\wrdElm
.

We just showed that
f
is a bijection between initial paths
{\mathrm{Pth}}_{\text{init}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})
and initial paths
{\mathrm{Pth}}_{\text{init}}({\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}})
.
∎

We want to state that a play in
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
is won by Eloise
iff
its image by
f
is also won by Eloise. To do so, we first characterize the plays in
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
that Eloise wins by linking them with an execution of
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
. To precise this link, we associate an assignment
\asgElm
to a play
\playElm
of
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
.

We define a function
g:{\mathrm{Play}}({\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}})\to{\mathrm{Val}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)})^{\omega}
as follow where
\playElm=(\playElm)_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is a play on
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
.

\begin{array}[]{l}g(\playElm)\in{\mathrm{Val}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)})^{\omega}\\
g(\playElm)_{t}\defeq\text{snd}(\playElm_{(t+1)\times({{\lvert\allowbreak{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\rvert}}+1)-1})\end{array}

Claim 18
.

The function
g
is a bijection and a play
\playElm
is won by Eloise
iff

g(\playElm)
is recognized by the automaton
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}^{\psi}}
.

Proof.

We first prove that
g
is a bijection by proving it is well defined and it is a surjection and an injection. By definition of the game
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
, for every
t\in{\mathbb{N}}
, it holds that
\playElm_{(t+1)\times({{\lvert\allowbreak{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\rvert}}+1)}
has the form
(q,{{\varnothing}})
. Thus, the previous positions necessarily has the form
(q^{\prime},\valElm)
with
\valElm\in{\mathrm{Val}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)})
. Then
g
is well defined.

To show that
g
is a bijection, we show that every word
w
is the image of exactly one play. Consider a word
w\in{\mathrm{Val}}({\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)})^{\omega}
. For every natural
t
and every position
(q,{{\varnothing}})
there exist a unique finite path of
{{\lvert\allowbreak{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\rvert}}
moves reaching the position
(q,w_{t})
, by definition of the game
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
. This path
\pthElm
is defined as
\pthElm_{0}=(q,{{\varnothing}})
and
\pthElm_{i+1}=(q,\valElm_{i+1})
with
\valElm_{i+1}=(w_{t})_{<i+1}
for every
i<{{\lvert\allowbreak{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\rvert}}
. Thanks to this construction, we can build step by step the full play, starting at
(q_{0},{{\varnothing}})
with
t=0
.

1.

From a state of the form
(q,{{\varnothing}})
, goes to
(q,w_{t})
in
{{\lvert\allowbreak{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\rvert}}
moves.

2.

The next move in necessarily from
(q,w_{t})
to
(\delta(q,w_{t}),{{\varnothing}})
.

3.

Go to
1)
with
t:=t+1

Steps
1)
and
2)
use
{{\lvert\allowbreak{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\rvert}}+1
moves. Positions of the form
(q,w_{t})
are reached at the end of the first step. Thus we have
\playElm_{(t+1)\times({{\lvert\allowbreak{\mathsf{ap}\!\left(\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\right)}\allowbreak\rvert}}+1)-1}=(q,w_{t})
.

Because of the parity condition of the game
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
, it is clear that a play
\playElm
is won by Eloise if and only if
g(\playElm)
is accepted by the automaton
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
.
∎

We now explicit the link between
f
and
g
: the word associated with a play in
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
is the sequence of observable position in the associated play in
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
.

Claim 19
.

Given a play
\playElm
in the game
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
, we have for every
t
natural number,
g(\playElm)_{t}={\mathsf{obs}}(f(\playElm))_{t}
.

Proof.

This claim derive directly from definitions of
f
,
g
and
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
.
∎

We now show that Eloise wins a play in
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}

iff
she wins the corresponding play in
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
.

Claim 20
.

The bijection
f
preserves the winner:
\playElm
is won by Eloise
in
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}

iff

f(\playElm)
is won by Eloise in
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
.

Proof.

Consider a play
\playElm
in the game
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
. Thanks to Claim
18
, we know that
\playElm
is won by Eloise
iff

g(\playElm)
is accepted by
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
which means that
{\mathsf{wrd}^{-1}}(g(\playElm))\models\psi
. Claim
19
assure that
g(\playElm)(n)={\mathsf{obs}}(f(\playElm))_{n}
. We can deduce that
{\mathsf{wrd}^{-1}}({\mathsf{obs}}(f(\playElm)))
define an assignment that satisfy
\psi
. Then
f(\playElm)
is won by Eloise.

The reciprocity is ensured by the bijective property of
f
(Claim
17
.)
∎

Because
f
is a bijection, we can derive a bijection between strategies on
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}
and strategies on
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
. It is not hard to see that a strategy for Eloise is winning
iff
its associated strategy is also winning. Thus Eloise wins
{\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}}

iff
she wins
{\mathcal{G}_{\qntElm}^{\psi}}
.

The automaton
A_{\psi}
has a size exponential in the size of
\psi
. The procedure to determinize in a parity automaton adds one exponential; thus
{{\lvert\allowbreak{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}\allowbreak\rvert}}={\mathrm{O}\!\left(\allowbreak 2^{2^{{{\lvert\allowbreak\psi\allowbreak\rvert}}}}\allowbreak\right)}
. The quantification game have a size in
{\mathrm{O}\!\left(\allowbreak 2^{{{\lvert\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\rvert}}}\allowbreak\right)}
. We conclude that the game constructed has a size in
{\mathrm{O}\!\left(\allowbreak 2^{{{\lvert\allowbreak\qntElm\allowbreak\rvert}}}2^{2^{{{\lvert\allowbreak\psi\allowbreak\rvert}}}}\allowbreak\right)}={\mathrm{O}\!\left(\allowbreak 2^{2^{{{\lvert\allowbreak\varphi\allowbreak\rvert}}}}\allowbreak\right)}
. The game has the same number of priorities as the automaton
{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
which is in
{\mathrm{O}\!\left(\allowbreak 2^{{{\lvert\allowbreak\psi\allowbreak\rvert}}}\allowbreak\right)}
.
∎
